By Markov’s inequality,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Pr \big [N^{(i)}_U = 0\big ]&\ge 1 - \mathbb {E}\big [N^{(i)}_U\big ] \\&= 1 - \vert U \vert \cdot 2^{-j} \\&\ge 1 - 2^{j-1} \cdot 2^{-j} = 1/2.\\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pr[NU(i)=0]≥1-E[NU(i)]=1-|U|·2-j≥1-2j-1·2-j=1/2.On the other hand,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Pr [N^{(i)}_W \ge 1]&= 1 - \left( 1 - 2^{-j} \right) ^{\vert W\vert } \\&\ge 1 - \left( 1 - 2^{-j} \right) ^{2^{j-2}} \\&\ge 1 - e^{-1/4} > 0.2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pr[NW(i)≥1]=1-1-2-j|W|≥1-1-2-jj-2≥1-e-1/4>0.2.Since U and W are disjoint, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{(i)}_U$$\end{document}NU(i) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{(i)}_W$$\end{document}NW(i) are independent random variables. Hence, with probability at least 0.1, the source \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_{ij}$$\end{document}sij is connected to w but not to u in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{ij} - F$$\end{document}Gij-F, and the answers for u and w given by the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{ij}(\cdot )$$\end{document}Lij(·)-labels are different. As the graphs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{G_{ij}\}_i$$\end{document}{Gij}i are formed independently, the probability there exists an i for which w is connected to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_{ij}$$\end{document}sij and u is disconnected from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_{ij}$$\end{document}sij is at least \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 - (0.9)^{\alpha \ln n / \ln (0.9)} = 1 - 1/n^\alpha $$\end{document}1-(0.9)αlnn/ln(0.9)=1-1/nα. In this case, the output is disconnected, as required. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□
